        if not ready:
            if "blocked" in k["status"]:
                return ("deadlock", prefix)
            if final is not None and not final(k["mem"]):
                return ("wrong end", prefix)
            return None
        if len(prefix) == max_steps:
            return None
        for i in ready:
            found = search(prefix + [i])
            if found is not None:
                return found
        return None

    return search([])


def add_job(mem, job):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    mem["jobs"].append(job)
    yield ("signal", "has_job")
    yield ("release", "m")


def take_job(mem):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    while not mem["jobs"]:
        yield ("wait", "has_job", "m")
    job = mem["jobs"].pop(0)
    yield ("release", "m")
    return job


def dispatcher(mem, jobs):
    for job in jobs:
        yield from add_job(mem, job)


def worker(mem, count):
    for _ in range(count):
        job = yield from take_job(mem)
        mem["done"].append(job)


def jobs_setup(jobs, counts):
    def setup():
        mem = {"jobs": [], "done": []}
        threads = [dispatcher(mem, jobs)] + [worker(mem, c) for c in counts]
        return make_monitor_kernel(mem, ["m"], ["has_job"], threads)
    return setup
--- hint
When a waiting worker is woken, is the job it was woken for still there by the time it gets the mutex back?
--- hint
Replace the `if` with a `while`, exactly what `cv.wait(lock, pred)` does in C++.
--- check test | Two workers, one job each
explore(jobs_setup(["a", "b"], [1, 1]), 300, None, lambda m: sorted(m["done"]) == ["a", "b"]) is None
--- check test | Three workers
explore(jobs_setup(["a", "b", "c"], [1, 1, 1]), 300, None, lambda m: sorted(m["done"]) == ["a", "b", "c"]) is None
--- check test | One worker taking everything, in order
explore(jobs_setup(["a", "b", "c"], [3]), 300, None, lambda m: m["done"] == ["a", "b", "c"]) is None
--- check test | More work wanted than offered: the explorer reports a deadlock, not a crash
(lambda r: r is not None and r[0] == "deadlock")(explore(jobs_setup(["a"], [1, 1]), 300))

+++ practice | A thread pool that knows when it is idle
--- task
Build the heart of a thread pool in the model. The shared state is `mem["jobs"]` (waiting jobs, oldest first), `mem["pending"]` (jobs submitted and **not yet finished**), `mem["stop"]`, and `mem["done"]` (finished jobs, filled in by the workers). The mutex is `"m"`; the condition variables are `"work"` (for workers) and `"idle"` (for threads waiting for the pool to empty). Write five generator functions, each taking the mutex first and releasing it before returning:

- `submit(mem, job)`: append the job, add 1 to `pending`, and wake one worker.
- `take(mem)`: wait while there are no jobs and the pool is not stopping. Return the oldest job, or `None` if there is none (the pool is stopping).
- `finish(mem)`: a worker calls this **after** it has run a job. Take 1 from `pending`; if that makes it 0, wake every thread waiting for idle.
- `wait_idle(mem)`: wait until `pending` is 0.
- `shutdown(mem)`: set `stop` and wake every worker.

The starter gives the kernel and explorer, and the harness: `pool_worker` (take, run the job by appending it to `done` in a step of its own, then `finish`), `boss` (for each batch: submit its jobs, `wait_idle`, then log how many jobs are done; finally shut down), `pool_setup(workers, batches)` and `pool_ok(mem, batches)`.
--- starter
def make_monitor_kernel(mem, locks, cvs, threads):
    return {
        "mem": mem,
        "lock": {name: None for name in locks},
        "lock_q": {name: [] for name in locks},
        "cv_q": {name: [] for name in cvs},
        "threads": list(threads),
        "status": ["ready"] * len(threads),
    }


def release(k, lock):
    if k["lock_q"][lock]:
        j = k["lock_q"][lock].pop(0)
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock"][lock] = None


def wake(k, cv):
    j, lock = k["cv_q"][cv].pop(0)
    if k["lock"][lock] is None:
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock_q"][lock].append(j)


def step(k, i):
    try:
        request = next(k["threads"][i])
    except StopIteration:
        k["status"][i] = "done"
        return
    if request is None:
        return
    op = request[0]
    if op == "acquire":
        lock = request[1]
        if k["lock"][lock] is None:
            k["lock"][lock] = i
        else:
            k["status"][i] = "blocked"
            k["lock_q"][lock].append(i)
    elif op == "release":
        if k["lock"][request[1]] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} released a lock it does not hold")
        release(k, request[1])
    elif op == "wait":
        cv, lock = request[1], request[2]
        if k["lock"][lock] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} waited without holding the lock")
        release(k, lock)
        k["status"][i] = "blocked"
        k["cv_q"][cv].append((i, lock))
    elif op == "signal":
        if k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])
    elif op == "broadcast":
        while k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])


def where(k):
    spots = []
    for g in k["threads"]:
        frames = []
        while g is not None and getattr(g, "gi_frame", None) is not None:
            f = g.gi_frame
            frames.append((f.f_lasti, repr(sorted(f.f_locals.items()))))
            g = g.gi_yieldfrom
        spots.append(frames)
    return repr((k["mem"], k["lock"], k["lock_q"], k["cv_q"], k["status"], spots))


def explore(setup, max_steps, bad=None, final=None):
    seen = set()

    def search(prefix):
        k = setup()
        for i in prefix:
            step(k, i)
        key = where(k)
        if key in seen:
            return None
        seen.add(key)
        if bad is not None and bad(k["mem"]):
            return ("bad", prefix)
        ready = [i for i, s in enumerate(k["status"]) if s == "ready"]
        if not ready:
            if "blocked" in k["status"]:
                return ("deadlock", prefix)
            if final is not None and not final(k["mem"]):
                return ("wrong end", prefix)
            return None
        if len(prefix) == max_steps:
            return None
        for i in ready:
            found = search(prefix + [i])
            if found is not None:
                return found
        return None

    return search([])


def pool_worker(mem):
    while True:
        job = yield from take(mem)
        if job is None:
            return
        mem["done"].append(job)
        yield
        yield from finish(mem)


def boss(mem, batches):
    for batch in batches:
        for job in batch:
            yield from submit(mem, job)
        yield from wait_idle(mem)
        mem["log"].append(("idle", len(mem["done"])))
    yield from shutdown(mem)


def pool_setup(workers, batches):
    def setup():
        mem = {"jobs": [], "pending": 0, "stop": False, "done": [], "log": []}
        threads = [boss(mem, batches)] + [pool_worker(mem) for _ in range(workers)]
        return make_monitor_kernel(mem, ["m"], ["work", "idle"], threads)
    return setup


def pool_ok(mem, batches):
    total = 0
    for (kind, finished), batch in zip(mem["log"], batches):
        total += len(batch)
        if finished != total:
            return False
    everything = sorted(job for batch in batches for job in batch)
    return len(mem["log"]) == len(batches) and sorted(mem["done"]) == everything


def submit(mem, job):
    raise NotImplementedError


def take(mem):
    raise NotImplementedError


def finish(mem):
    raise NotImplementedError


def wait_idle(mem):
    raise NotImplementedError


def shutdown(mem):
    raise NotImplementedError
--- solution
def make_monitor_kernel(mem, locks, cvs, threads):
    return {
        "mem": mem,
        "lock": {name: None for name in locks},
        "lock_q": {name: [] for name in locks},
        "cv_q": {name: [] for name in cvs},
        "threads": list(threads),
        "status": ["ready"] * len(threads),
    }


def release(k, lock):
    if k["lock_q"][lock]:
        j = k["lock_q"][lock].pop(0)
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock"][lock] = None


def wake(k, cv):
    j, lock = k["cv_q"][cv].pop(0)
    if k["lock"][lock] is None:
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock_q"][lock].append(j)


def step(k, i):
    try:
        request = next(k["threads"][i])
    except StopIteration:
        k["status"][i] = "done"
        return
    if request is None:
        return
    op = request[0]
    if op == "acquire":
        lock = request[1]
        if k["lock"][lock] is None:
            k["lock"][lock] = i
        else:
            k["status"][i] = "blocked"
            k["lock_q"][lock].append(i)
    elif op == "release":
        if k["lock"][request[1]] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} released a lock it does not hold")
        release(k, request[1])
    elif op == "wait":
        cv, lock = request[1], request[2]
        if k["lock"][lock] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} waited without holding the lock")
        release(k, lock)
        k["status"][i] = "blocked"
        k["cv_q"][cv].append((i, lock))
    elif op == "signal":
        if k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])
    elif op == "broadcast":
        while k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])


def where(k):
    spots = []
    for g in k["threads"]:
        frames = []
        while g is not None and getattr(g, "gi_frame", None) is not None:
            f = g.gi_frame
            frames.append((f.f_lasti, repr(sorted(f.f_locals.items()))))
            g = g.gi_yieldfrom
        spots.append(frames)
    return repr((k["mem"], k["lock"], k["lock_q"], k["cv_q"], k["status"], spots))


def explore(setup, max_steps, bad=None, final=None):
    seen = set()

    def search(prefix):
        k = setup()
        for i in prefix:
            step(k, i)
        key = where(k)
        if key in seen:
            return None
        seen.add(key)
        if bad is not None and bad(k["mem"]):
            return ("bad", prefix)
        ready = [i for i, s in enumerate(k["status"]) if s == "ready"]
        if not ready:
            if "blocked" in k["status"]:
                return ("deadlock", prefix)
            if final is not None and not final(k["mem"]):
                return ("wrong end", prefix)
            return None
        if len(prefix) == max_steps:
            return None
        for i in ready:
            found = search(prefix + [i])
            if found is not None:
                return found
        return None

    return search([])


def pool_worker(mem):
    while True:
        job = yield from take(mem)
        if job is None:
            return
        mem["done"].append(job)
        yield
        yield from finish(mem)


def boss(mem, batches):
    for batch in batches:
        for job in batch:
            yield from submit(mem, job)
        yield from wait_idle(mem)
        mem["log"].append(("idle", len(mem["done"])))
    yield from shutdown(mem)


def pool_setup(workers, batches):
    def setup():
        mem = {"jobs": [], "pending": 0, "stop": False, "done": [], "log": []}
        threads = [boss(mem, batches)] + [pool_worker(mem) for _ in range(workers)]
        return make_monitor_kernel(mem, ["m"], ["work", "idle"], threads)
    return setup


def pool_ok(mem, batches):
    total = 0
    for (kind, finished), batch in zip(mem["log"], batches):
        total += len(batch)
        if finished != total:
            return False
    everything = sorted(job for batch in batches for job in batch)
    return len(mem["log"]) == len(batches) and sorted(mem["done"]) == everything


def submit(mem, job):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    mem["jobs"].append(job)
    mem["pending"] += 1
    yield ("signal", "work")
    yield ("release", "m")


def take(mem):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    while not mem["jobs"] and not mem["stop"]:
        yield ("wait", "work", "m")
    if not mem["jobs"]:
        yield ("release", "m")
        return None
    job = mem["jobs"].pop(0)
    yield ("release", "m")
    return job


def finish(mem):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    mem["pending"] -= 1
    if mem["pending"] == 0:
        yield ("broadcast", "idle")
    yield ("release", "m")


def wait_idle(mem):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    while mem["pending"] > 0:
        yield ("wait", "idle", "m")
    yield ("release", "m")


def shutdown(mem):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    mem["stop"] = True
    yield ("broadcast", "work")
    yield ("release", "m")
--- hint
`pending` must count jobs that are queued **or running**. It goes up in `submit` and down only in `finish`, never in `take`: otherwise `wait_idle` can return while a worker is still in the middle of a job.
--- hint
`take` is the queue's `pop` with `stop` in place of `closed`. `finish` wakes idle-waiters with `broadcast` when `pending` reaches 0, and `shutdown` wakes every worker with `broadcast`, as `close` did.
--- check test | One worker, one batch
explore(pool_setup(1, [["a", "b"]]), 400, None, lambda m: pool_ok(m, [["a", "b"]])) is None
--- check test | Two workers: wait_idle must not return while a job is still running
explore(pool_setup(2, [["a", "b"]]), 400, None, lambda m: pool_ok(m, [["a", "b"]])) is None
?? A "wrong end" means the boss logged idle too early: pending must drop only when a job is finished, not when it is taken.
--- check test | Two batches, two workers
explore(pool_setup(2, [["a", "b"], ["c"]]), 400, None, lambda m: pool_ok(m, [["a", "b"], ["c"]])) is None
--- check test | An empty batch: wait_idle returns at once
explore(pool_setup(1, [[], ["x"]]), 400, None, lambda m: pool_ok(m, [[], ["x"]])) is None
--- check test | Shutdown with idle workers asleep ends every thread
explore(pool_setup(3, [["j"]]), 400, None, lambda m: m["stop"] and m["jobs"] == [] and m["pending"] == 0) is None

=== os-gate | Operating Systems: mastery gate
--- teach
The gate covers the whole course: system calls and processes, scheduling, threads and locks, semaphores and monitors, deadlock, memory allocation, paging and replacement, file systems and journaling, disks, virtualisation and containers, and real threads. Every problem combines at least two of those ideas, and none of them appeared in the practice. You have 112 minutes for 10 problems and 12 questions, with no hints. To get ready, redo the practice problems of the lessons that felt hard, and make sure you can argue why each mechanism is correct, not only run it.
--- gate
pass 7
questions 10
minutes 112

+++ problem | System calls on a process table
--- task
Model how a kernel answers process system calls. Write `run_calls(calls)`. At the start the only process is init, pid 1 (its parent is 0). `calls` is a list of tuples `(pid, name, *args)`, handled in order. Return a pair: the list of results, one per call, and the sorted list of pids that are zombies at the end.

- A call from a pid that does not exist, or is a zombie, returns `-ESRCH` (−3) and does nothing.
- `("fork",)`: create a child of the caller with the next unused pid (2, 3, …). The result is the child's pid.
- `("getppid",)`: the caller's parent's pid.
- `("exit", status)`: the caller becomes a zombie holding `status`; every child it has (alive or zombie) gets init as its new parent. The result is `None`. Init reaps any zombie whose parent is init at once, so such zombies disappear immediately.
- `("wait",)`: a non-blocking `waitpid(-1, ..., WNOHANG)`. If the caller has a zombie child, reap the one with the lowest pid and return `(pid, status)`; the process disappears. Otherwise, if it has living children, return 0. If it has no children at all, return `-ECHILD` (−10).
--- starter
ECHILD = 10
ESRCH = 3


def run_calls(calls):
    return [], []
--- solution
ECHILD = 10
ESRCH = 3


def run_calls(calls):
    parent = {1: 0}
    zombie = {}
    next_pid = 2
    results = []

    def reap_orphans():
        for z in sorted(zombie):
            if parent[z] == 1:
                del zombie[z]
                del parent[z]

    for pid, call, *args in calls:
        if pid not in parent or pid in zombie:
            results.append(-ESRCH)
            continue
        if call == "fork":
            parent[next_pid] = pid
            results.append(next_pid)
            next_pid += 1
        elif call == "getppid":
            results.append(parent[pid])
        elif call == "exit":
            zombie[pid] = args[0]
            for child in parent:
                if parent[child] == pid:
                    parent[child] = 1
            reap_orphans()
            results.append(None)
        elif call == "wait":
            children = sorted(c for c in parent if parent[c] == pid)
            dead = [c for c in children if c in zombie]
            if dead:
                status = zombie.pop(dead[0])
                del parent[dead[0]]
                results.append((dead[0], status))
            elif children:
                results.append(0)
            else:
                results.append(-ECHILD)
    return results, sorted(zombie)
--- check case | Orphans are adopted by init, and init reaps its zombies
run_calls([(1, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (3, "getppid"), (2, "exit", 7), (3, "getppid"), (1, "wait"), (3, "exit", 0), (1, "wait")])
=> ([2, 3, 2, None, 1, 0, None, -10], [])
--- check case | A parent reaps its zombie child once; then there is nothing left
run_calls([(1, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (3, "exit", 4), (2, "wait"), (2, "wait"), (9, "fork")])
=> ([2, 3, None, (3, 4), -10, -3], [])
--- check case | Zombies are reaped lowest pid first, and a zombie cannot make calls
run_calls([(1, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (4, "exit", 2), (3, "exit", 1), (3, "fork"), (2, "wait"), (2, "wait"), (2, "wait")])
=> ([2, 3, 4, None, None, -3, (3, 1), (4, 2), -10], [])
--- check case | A parent reaps its child; init has a living child, so its wait gives 0
run_calls([(1, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (3, "exit", 9), (2, "wait"), (1, "wait")])
=> ([2, 3, None, (3, 9), 0], [])
--- check case | Zombies stay zombies until someone waits
run_calls([(1, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (3, "exit", 1), (4, "exit", 5)])
=> ([2, 3, 4, None, None], [3, 4])
--- check case | When a parent exits, its zombie children go to init and vanish
run_calls([(1, "fork"), (2, "fork"), (3, "exit", 5), (2, "exit", 0), (1, "wait")])
=> ([2, 3, None, None, -10], [])
--- check case | No calls
run_calls([])
=> ([], [])

+++ problem | Round robin with I/O bursts
--- task
Simulate round robin scheduling of jobs that alternate between computing and waiting for I/O. Write `rr_io(jobs, quantum)`. Each job is `(arrival, bursts)`, where `bursts` is a list of odd length: CPU time, I/O time, CPU time, …, ending with CPU time. There is one CPU, context switches are free, and every job's I/O proceeds on its own, all in parallel.

- The ready queue is first in, first out. A job runs for `min(quantum, remaining CPU burst)`.
- If its burst ends and more bursts follow, it starts its I/O at once and rejoins the ready queue when the I/O ends. If it was its last burst, the job is complete.
- If the quantum runs out first, it goes to the back of the ready queue, **after** every job that arrived or finished its I/O at or before that moment.
- Jobs joining the queue at the same moment join in increasing order of time, then of job index.
- When the queue is empty the CPU is idle until the next job becomes ready.

Return `(completion, idle)`: the list of completion times in job order, and the total time the CPU was idle before the last job completed.
--- starter
def rr_io(jobs, quantum):
    return [sum(b) for _, b in jobs], 0
--- solution
from collections import deque


def rr_io(jobs, quantum):
    left = [list(bursts) for _, bursts in jobs]
    done = [None] * len(jobs)
    events = [(arrival, i) for i, (arrival, _) in enumerate(jobs)]
    ready = deque()
    now = 0
    idle = 0

    def admit():
        events.sort()
        while events and events[0][0] <= now:
            ready.append(events.pop(0)[1])

    while None in done:
        admit()
        if not ready:
            nxt = min(t for t, _ in events)
            idle += nxt - now
            now = nxt
            continue
        i = ready.popleft()
        run = min(quantum, left[i][0])
        now += run
        left[i][0] -= run
        if left[i][0] == 0:
            left[i].pop(0)
            if left[i]:
                events.append((now + left[i].pop(0), i))
            else:
                done[i] = now
            admit()
        else:
            admit()
            ready.append(i)
    return done, idle
--- check case | One CPU-bound job
rr_io([(0, [5])], 2)
=> ([5], 0)
--- check case | I/O leaves the CPU idle when nobody else is ready
rr_io([(0, [3, 4, 2]), (0, [4])], 2)
=> ([11, 7], 2)
--- check case | A late arrival after an idle gap
rr_io([(0, [2, 10, 1]), (1, [6]), (20, [1])], 4)
=> ([13, 8, 21], 11)
--- check case | A job arriving as another's quantum ends goes first
rr_io([(0, [4]), (2, [2])], 2)
=> ([6, 4], 0)
--- check case | A single job with I/O: the CPU idles while it waits
rr_io([(0, [1, 2, 1])], 3)
=> ([4], 2)
--- check case | Three jobs, overlapping I/O
rr_io([(0, [2, 3, 2]), (0, [1, 1, 1]), (1, [3])], 2)
=> ([8, 6, 9], 0)
--- check case | No jobs
rr_io([], 3)
=> ([], 0)

+++ problem | How often can a lock let others cut in?
--- task
**Bounded waiting** says: once a thread has started trying to enter its critical section, other threads can enter only a bounded number of times before it does. Measure this by exhaustive search, with the generator threads of lessons 5 and 6 (the code between two `yield`s is one atomic step, and a step that raises `StopIteration` finishes the thread).

The starter gives three lock workers (leave them as they are): a test-and-set spinlock, Peterson's algorithm and a ticket lock. Each logs `("try", me)` in the step where it starts trying, and `("enter", me)` in the step where it enters. `make_setup(worker, n, rounds)` returns a setup function giving fresh memory and `n` threads, each entering `rounds` times.

1. `bypass(log)`: the largest number of `"enter"` entries by other threads between some thread's `"try"` and its next `"enter"` (or the end of the log, if it never entered).
2. `max_bypass(setup, max_steps)`: try every schedule of at most `max_steps` steps (at each point any unfinished thread may step, until all are finished or `max_steps` steps have run), replaying each from a fresh `setup()`, and return the largest `bypass` of any schedule's log.
--- starter
def make_setup(worker, n, rounds):
    def setup():
        mem = {"log": [], "flag": [False] * n, "turn": 0, "lock": False, "next": 0, "serving": 0}
        return mem, [worker(mem, i, rounds) for i in range(n)]
    return setup


def tas_worker(mem, me, rounds):
    for _ in range(rounds):
        mem["log"].append(("try", me))
        while mem["lock"]:
            yield
        mem["lock"] = True
        yield
        mem["log"].append(("enter", me))
        yield
        mem["lock"] = False
        yield


def peterson_worker(mem, me, rounds):
    other = 1 - me
    for _ in range(rounds):
        mem["log"].append(("try", me))
        mem["flag"][me] = True
        yield
        mem["turn"] = other
        yield
        while mem["flag"][other] and mem["turn"] == other:
            yield
        mem["log"].append(("enter", me))
        yield
        mem["flag"][me] = False
        yield


def ticket_worker(mem, me, rounds):
    for _ in range(rounds):
        mem["log"].append(("try", me))
        ticket = mem["next"]
        mem["next"] += 1
        yield
        while mem["serving"] != ticket:
            yield
        mem["log"].append(("enter", me))
        yield
        mem["serving"] += 1
        yield


def bypass(log):
    return 0


def max_bypass(setup, max_steps):
    return 0
--- solution
def make_setup(worker, n, rounds):
    def setup():
        mem = {"log": [], "flag": [False] * n, "turn": 0, "lock": False, "next": 0, "serving": 0}
        return mem, [worker(mem, i, rounds) for i in range(n)]
    return setup


def tas_worker(mem, me, rounds):
    for _ in range(rounds):
        mem["log"].append(("try", me))
        while mem["lock"]:
            yield
        mem["lock"] = True
        yield
        mem["log"].append(("enter", me))
        yield
        mem["lock"] = False
        yield


def peterson_worker(mem, me, rounds):
    other = 1 - me
    for _ in range(rounds):
        mem["log"].append(("try", me))
        mem["flag"][me] = True
        yield
        mem["turn"] = other
        yield
        while mem["flag"][other] and mem["turn"] == other:
            yield
        mem["log"].append(("enter", me))
        yield
        mem["flag"][me] = False
        yield


def ticket_worker(mem, me, rounds):
    for _ in range(rounds):
        mem["log"].append(("try", me))
        ticket = mem["next"]
        mem["next"] += 1
        yield
        while mem["serving"] != ticket:
            yield
        mem["log"].append(("enter", me))
        yield
        mem["serving"] += 1
        yield


def bypass(log):
    worst = 0
    waiting = {}
    for kind, who in log:
        if kind == "try":
            waiting[who] = 0
        else:
            waiting.pop(who, None)
            for other in waiting:
                waiting[other] += 1
                worst = max(worst, waiting[other])
    return worst


def max_bypass(setup, max_steps):
    best = 0

    def search(prefix):
        nonlocal best
        mem, threads = setup()
        done = set()
        for i in prefix:
            try:
                next(threads[i])
            except StopIteration:
                done.add(i)
        live = [i for i in range(len(threads)) if i not in done]
        if not live or len(prefix) == max_steps:
            best = max(best, bypass(mem["log"]))
            return
        for i in live:
            search(prefix + [i])

    search([])
    return best
--- check case | bypass on hand-made logs
(bypass([("try", 0), ("try", 1), ("enter", 1), ("try", 1), ("enter", 1), ("enter", 0)]), bypass([("try", 0), ("enter", 0)]), bypass([]), bypass([("try", 0), ("try", 1), ("enter", 1)]))
=> (2, 0, 0, 1)
--- check case | Three threads: every waiter counts the others' entries
bypass([("try", 0), ("try", 1), ("try", 2), ("enter", 2), ("enter", 1), ("try", 2), ("enter", 2), ("enter", 0)])
=> 3
--- check case | Test-and-set lets the other thread in every time it tries
[max_bypass(make_setup(tas_worker, 2, r), 14) for r in (1, 2, 3)]
=> [1, 2, 3]
--- check case | Peterson and the ticket lock bound the bypass by 1 for two threads
(max_bypass(make_setup(peterson_worker, 2, 3), 14), max_bypass(make_setup(ticket_worker, 2, 3), 14))
=> (1, 1)
--- check case | The ticket lock with three threads: n - 1
max_bypass(make_setup(ticket_worker, 3, 1), 9)
=> 2
--- check case | Too few steps to enter at all
max_bypass(make_setup(peterson_worker, 2, 1), 2)
=> 0

+++ problem | A one-lane bridge
--- task
A bridge has one lane. Cars going `"N"` and cars going `"S"` may never be on it at the same time, and at most `mem["cap"]` cars may be on it at once. Write the monitor that enforces this, in the kernel model of lessons 7 and 17: the mutex is `"m"` and the condition variable is `"change"`. `mem["on"]` is how many cars are on the bridge and `mem["dir"]` the direction they are going (`None` when it is empty).

- `enter_bridge(mem, d)`: wait until the bridge is empty, or it carries cars going `d` and has room. Then count yourself on and set the direction to `d`.
- `leave_bridge(mem, d)`: count yourself off; if the bridge is now empty, set the direction to `None`. Wake the threads that may now be able to enter.

Both are generator functions that take the mutex first and release it before returning. The starter gives the kernel and explorer, `bridge_setup(cap, directions)` (one car thread per direction in the list; each enters, is on the bridge for one step, and leaves), and `unsafe(mem)`, true when cars of both directions, or more than `cap` cars, are on the bridge.
--- starter
def make_monitor_kernel(mem, locks, cvs, threads):
    return {
        "mem": mem,
        "lock": {name: None for name in locks},
        "lock_q": {name: [] for name in locks},
        "cv_q": {name: [] for name in cvs},
        "threads": list(threads),
        "status": ["ready"] * len(threads),
    }


def release(k, lock):
    if k["lock_q"][lock]:
        j = k["lock_q"][lock].pop(0)
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock"][lock] = None


def wake(k, cv):
    j, lock = k["cv_q"][cv].pop(0)
    if k["lock"][lock] is None:
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock_q"][lock].append(j)


def step(k, i):
    try:
        request = next(k["threads"][i])
    except StopIteration:
        k["status"][i] = "done"
        return
    if request is None:
        return
    op = request[0]
    if op == "acquire":
        lock = request[1]
        if k["lock"][lock] is None:
            k["lock"][lock] = i
        else:
            k["status"][i] = "blocked"
            k["lock_q"][lock].append(i)
    elif op == "release":
        if k["lock"][request[1]] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} released a lock it does not hold")
        release(k, request[1])
    elif op == "wait":
        cv, lock = request[1], request[2]
        if k["lock"][lock] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} waited without holding the lock")
        release(k, lock)
        k["status"][i] = "blocked"
        k["cv_q"][cv].append((i, lock))
    elif op == "signal":
        if k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])
    elif op == "broadcast":
        while k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])


def where(k):
    spots = []
    for g in k["threads"]:
        frames = []
        while g is not None and getattr(g, "gi_frame", None) is not None:
            f = g.gi_frame
            frames.append((f.f_lasti, repr(sorted(f.f_locals.items()))))
            g = g.gi_yieldfrom
        spots.append(frames)
    return repr((k["mem"], k["lock"], k["lock_q"], k["cv_q"], k["status"], spots))


def explore(setup, max_steps, bad=None, final=None):
    seen = set()

    def search(prefix):
        k = setup()
        for i in prefix:
            step(k, i)
        key = where(k)
        if key in seen:
            return None
        seen.add(key)
        if bad is not None and bad(k["mem"]):
            return ("bad", prefix)
        ready = [i for i, s in enumerate(k["status"]) if s == "ready"]
        if not ready:
            if "blocked" in k["status"]:
                return ("deadlock", prefix)
            if final is not None and not final(k["mem"]):
                return ("wrong end", prefix)
            return None
        if len(prefix) == max_steps:
            return None
        for i in ready:
            found = search(prefix + [i])
            if found is not None:
                return found
        return None

    return search([])


def car(mem, d):
    yield from enter_bridge(mem, d)
    mem["cars"].append(d)
    yield
    mem["cars"].remove(d)
    yield from leave_bridge(mem, d)
    mem["crossed"].append(d)


def bridge_setup(cap, directions):
    def setup():
        mem = {"cap": cap, "on": 0, "dir": None, "cars": [], "crossed": []}
        return make_monitor_kernel(mem, ["m"], ["change"], [car(mem, d) for d in directions])
    return setup


def unsafe(mem):
    return len(set(mem["cars"])) > 1 or len(mem["cars"]) > mem["cap"]


def enter_bridge(mem, d):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    mem["on"] += 1
    mem["dir"] = d
    yield ("release", "m")


def leave_bridge(mem, d):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    mem["on"] -= 1
    yield ("release", "m")
--- solution
def make_monitor_kernel(mem, locks, cvs, threads):
    return {
        "mem": mem,
        "lock": {name: None for name in locks},
        "lock_q": {name: [] for name in locks},
        "cv_q": {name: [] for name in cvs},
        "threads": list(threads),
        "status": ["ready"] * len(threads),
    }


def release(k, lock):
    if k["lock_q"][lock]:
        j = k["lock_q"][lock].pop(0)
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock"][lock] = None


def wake(k, cv):
    j, lock = k["cv_q"][cv].pop(0)
    if k["lock"][lock] is None:
        k["lock"][lock] = j
        k["status"][j] = "ready"
    else:
        k["lock_q"][lock].append(j)


def step(k, i):
    try:
        request = next(k["threads"][i])
    except StopIteration:
        k["status"][i] = "done"
        return
    if request is None:
        return
    op = request[0]
    if op == "acquire":
        lock = request[1]
        if k["lock"][lock] is None:
            k["lock"][lock] = i
        else:
            k["status"][i] = "blocked"
            k["lock_q"][lock].append(i)
    elif op == "release":
        if k["lock"][request[1]] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} released a lock it does not hold")
        release(k, request[1])
    elif op == "wait":
        cv, lock = request[1], request[2]
        if k["lock"][lock] != i:
            raise RuntimeError(f"thread {i} waited without holding the lock")
        release(k, lock)
        k["status"][i] = "blocked"
        k["cv_q"][cv].append((i, lock))
    elif op == "signal":
        if k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])
    elif op == "broadcast":
        while k["cv_q"][request[1]]:
            wake(k, request[1])


def where(k):
    spots = []
    for g in k["threads"]:
        frames = []
        while g is not None and getattr(g, "gi_frame", None) is not None:
            f = g.gi_frame
            frames.append((f.f_lasti, repr(sorted(f.f_locals.items()))))
            g = g.gi_yieldfrom
        spots.append(frames)
    return repr((k["mem"], k["lock"], k["lock_q"], k["cv_q"], k["status"], spots))


def explore(setup, max_steps, bad=None, final=None):
    seen = set()

    def search(prefix):
        k = setup()
        for i in prefix:
            step(k, i)
        key = where(k)
        if key in seen:
            return None
        seen.add(key)
        if bad is not None and bad(k["mem"]):
            return ("bad", prefix)
        ready = [i for i, s in enumerate(k["status"]) if s == "ready"]
        if not ready:
            if "blocked" in k["status"]:
                return ("deadlock", prefix)
            if final is not None and not final(k["mem"]):
                return ("wrong end", prefix)
            return None
        if len(prefix) == max_steps:
            return None
        for i in ready:
            found = search(prefix + [i])
            if found is not None:
                return found
        return None

    return search([])


def car(mem, d):
    yield from enter_bridge(mem, d)
    mem["cars"].append(d)
    yield
    mem["cars"].remove(d)
    yield from leave_bridge(mem, d)
    mem["crossed"].append(d)


def bridge_setup(cap, directions):
    def setup():
        mem = {"cap": cap, "on": 0, "dir": None, "cars": [], "crossed": []}
        return make_monitor_kernel(mem, ["m"], ["change"], [car(mem, d) for d in directions])
    return setup


def unsafe(mem):
    return len(set(mem["cars"])) > 1 or len(mem["cars"]) > mem["cap"]


def enter_bridge(mem, d):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    while not (mem["on"] == 0 or (mem["dir"] == d and mem["on"] < mem["cap"])):
        yield ("wait", "change", "m")
    mem["on"] += 1
    mem["dir"] = d
    yield ("release", "m")


def leave_bridge(mem, d):
    yield ("acquire", "m")
    mem["on"] -= 1
    if mem["on"] == 0:
        mem["dir"] = None
    yield ("broadcast", "change")
    yield ("release", "m")
--- check test | One car each way, capacity 1
explore(bridge_setup(1, ["N", "S"]), 400, unsafe, lambda m: len(m["crossed"]) == 2 and m["on"] == 0 and m["dir"] is None) is None
--- check test | Two cars north and one south, capacity 2
explore(bridge_setup(2, ["N", "N", "S"]), 400, unsafe, lambda m: len(m["crossed"]) == 3 and m["on"] == 0 and m["dir"] is None) is None
--- check test | Alternating directions, capacity 2
explore(bridge_setup(2, ["N", "S", "N", "S"]), 400, unsafe, lambda m: len(m["crossed"]) == 4 and m["on"] == 0) is None
--- check test | Capacity 1 with four cars
explore(bridge_setup(1, ["N", "N", "S", "S"]), 400, unsafe, lambda m: len(m["crossed"]) == 4 and m["dir"] is None) is None
--- check test | Cars going the same way really do share the bridge
(lambda r: r is not None and r[0] == "bad")(explore(bridge_setup(2, ["N", "N"]), 400, lambda m: len(m["cars"]) > 1))

+++ problem | The banker, request by request
--- task
Run the banker's algorithm of lesson 9 over a stream of events, with a queue for requests that must wait. Write `bank(available, maximum, allocation, events)`. `maximum` and `allocation` are lists of per-process resource vectors; `available` is the vector of free instances. Do not change the arguments. Each event is `("req", p, vector)` or `("rel", p, vector)`.

- A request asking for more than the process's remaining need (maximum minus allocation) in any resource gives `"error"` and changes nothing.
- Otherwise, grant it if there are enough free instances **and** the state after granting is safe (the safety algorithm of lesson 9). Log `"granted"`.
- If not, the request joins the back of a waiting queue, and the log says `"waits"`.
- A release returns the vector (always valid) to the free pool. Then go through the waiting queue once, in order, granting every request that can now be granted safely; the others keep their places. Log the list of processes whose requests were granted by this release, in the order granted.

Return `(log, available)`: the log, one entry per event, and the free vector at the end.
--- starter
def bank(available, maximum, allocation, events):
    return ["granted"] * len(events), list(available)
--- solution
def safe(available, maximum, allocation):
    work = list(available)
    finished = [False] * len(maximum)
    progress = True
    while progress:
        progress = False
        for p in range(len(maximum)):
            need = [m - a for m, a in zip(maximum[p], allocation[p])]
            if not finished[p] and all(n <= w for n, w in zip(need, work)):
                work = [w + a for w, a in zip(work, allocation[p])]
                finished[p] = True
                progress = True
    return all(finished)


def bank(available, maximum, allocation, events):
    available = list(available)
    allocation = [list(row) for row in allocation]
    waiting = []
    log = []

    def try_grant(p, req):
        if any(r > a for r, a in zip(req, available)):
            return False
        avail = [a - r for a, r in zip(available, req)]
        alloc = [list(row) for row in allocation]
        alloc[p] = [a + r for a, r in zip(alloc[p], req)]
        if not safe(avail, maximum, alloc):
            return False
        available[:] = avail
        allocation[p] = alloc[p]
        return True

    for kind, p, vec in events:
        if kind == "req":
            need = [m - a for m, a in zip(maximum[p], allocation[p])]
            if any(r > n for r, n in zip(vec, need)):
                log.append("error")
            elif try_grant(p, vec):
                log.append("granted")
            else:
                waiting.append((p, vec))
                log.append("waits")
        else:
            allocation[p] = [a - r for a, r in zip(allocation[p], vec)]
            available[:] = [a + r for a, r in zip(available, vec)]
            woke = []
            still = []
            for q, req in waiting:
                if try_grant(q, req):
                    woke.append(q)
                else:
                    still.append((q, req))
            waiting = still
            log.append(woke)
    return log, available
--- check case | The textbook state: a safe grant, an unsafe wait, a release that wakes one
bank([3, 3, 2], [[7, 5, 3], [3, 2, 2], [9, 0, 2], [2, 2, 2], [4, 3, 3]], [[0, 1, 0], [2, 0, 0], [3, 0, 2], [2, 1, 1], [0, 0, 2]], [("req", 1, [1, 0, 2]), ("req", 4, [3, 3, 0]), ("req", 0, [0, 2, 0]), ("rel", 1, [3, 0, 2]), ("req", 0, [0, 1, 0]), ("req", 2, [7, 0, 0])])
=> (["granted", "waits", "waits", [4], "waits", "error"], [2, 0, 2])
--- check case | Enough instances but unsafe: the request waits
bank([1], [[2], [2]], [[1], [0]], [("req", 1, [1])])
=> (["waits"], [1])
--- check case | A waiter is granted only when a release makes room for it
bank([1], [[2], [2], [1]], [[1], [0], [0]], [("req", 1, [2]), ("req", 2, [1]), ("rel", 0, [1]), ("rel", 2, [1])])
=> (["waits", "granted", [], [1]], [0])
--- check test | The arguments are not changed
(lambda av, mx, al: (bank(av, mx, al, [("req", 0, [1])]), av, al) == ((["granted"], [0]), [1], [[0]]))([1], [[1]], [[0]])
--- check case | No events
bank([2, 2], [[1, 1]], [[0, 0]], [])
=> ([], [2, 2])

+++ problem | An extent allocator for a file system
--- task
Allocate disk blocks to files in **extents** (runs of consecutive blocks), with the best-fit policy of lesson 10. Write a class `ExtentFS`. `ExtentFS(n)` has blocks 0 to `n - 1`, all free.

- `grow(name, count)`: give file `name` (created if new) `count` more blocks, in this order of preference. First, extend the file in place: take free blocks directly after its last block, one after another, as far as they go. Then, while blocks are still needed: if some free run is long enough for all of them, take the start of the **smallest** such run (ties: the lowest start); otherwise take the **largest** free run whole (ties: the lowest start) and continue. If there are fewer than `count` free blocks in total, allocate nothing and return `False`; otherwise return `True`.
- `delete(name)`: free all of the file's blocks.
- `extents(name)`: the file's blocks in file order, as a list of `(start, length)` pairs, where each pair is a maximal run of consecutive blocks.
- `free_runs()`: the free space as a sorted list of `(start, length)` for each maximal free run.
--- starter
class ExtentFS:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n

    def grow(self, name, count):
        return True

    def delete(self, name):
        pass

    def extents(self, name):
        return []

    def free_runs(self):
        return [(0, self.n)]
--- solution
class ExtentFS:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.owner = [None] * n
        self.blocks = {}

    def free_runs(self):
        runs = []
        start = None
        for b, who in enumerate(self.owner + ["end"]):
            if who is None and start is None:
                start = b
            elif who is not None and start is not None:
                runs.append((start, b - start))
                start = None
        return runs

    def take(self, name, start, count):
        for b in range(start, start + count):
            self.owner[b] = name
            self.blocks[name].append(b)

    def grow(self, name, count):
        if self.owner.count(None) < count:
            return False
        self.blocks.setdefault(name, [])
        if self.blocks[name]:
            b = self.blocks[name][-1] + 1
            while count and b < len(self.owner) and self.owner[b] is None:
                self.take(name, b, 1)
                b += 1
                count -= 1
        while count:
            runs = self.free_runs()
            fits = [r for r in runs if r[1] >= count]
            if fits:
                start, _ = min(fits, key=lambda r: (r[1], r[0]))
                self.take(name, start, count)
                count = 0
            else:
                start, length = max(runs, key=lambda r: (r[1], -r[0]))
                self.take(name, start, length)
                count -= length
        return True

    def delete(self, name):
        for b in self.blocks.pop(name, []):
            self.owner[b] = None

    def extents(self, name):
        out = []
        for b in self.blocks.get(name, []):
            if out and out[-1][0] + out[-1][1] == b:
                out[-1] = (out[-1][0], out[-1][1] + 1)
            else:
                out.append((b, 1))
        return out
--- check test | New files go into the best-fitting run
(lambda f: (f.grow("a", 4), f.grow("b", 3), f.grow("c", 5), f.extents("a"), f.extents("c"), f.free_runs()) == (True, True, True, [(0, 4)], [(7, 5)], [(12, 8)]))(ExtentFS(20))
--- check test | Growing in place first, after a neighbour is deleted
(lambda f: (f.grow("a", 4), f.grow("b", 3), f.grow("c", 5), f.delete("b"), f.free_runs(), f.grow("a", 2), f.extents("a"), f.free_runs())[4:] == ([(4, 3), (12, 8)], True, [(0, 6)], [(6, 1), (12, 8)]))(ExtentFS(20))
--- check test | Best fit picks the smallest run that fits, not the first
(lambda f: (f.grow("x", 2), f.grow("y", 5), f.grow("z", 1), f.grow("w", 3), f.delete("x"), f.delete("z"), f.grow("v", 1), f.extents("v"))[7] == [(7, 1)])(ExtentFS(12))
--- check test | Nothing fits: the largest runs are used, in file order
(lambda f: (f.grow("a", 4), f.grow("b", 3), f.grow("c", 5), f.delete("b"), f.grow("a", 2), f.grow("d", 6), f.grow("e", 3), f.extents("e"), f.free_runs(), f.grow("e", 1))[7:] == ([(18, 2), (6, 1)], [], False))(ExtentFS(20))
--- check test | Too few free blocks: nothing is allocated
(lambda f: (f.grow("a", 8), f.grow("b", 3), f.free_runs(), f.extents("b")) == (True, False, [(8, 2)], []))(ExtentFS(10))
--- check test | Extending in place stops at the end of the disk
(lambda f: (f.grow("a", 3), f.grow("pad", 1), f.delete("a"), f.grow("b", 2), f.grow("b", 3), f.extents("b"))[5] == [(4, 2), (0, 3)])(ExtentFS(6))
--- check test | Deleting everything leaves one free run
(lambda f: (f.grow("a", 3), f.grow("b", 3), f.delete("a"), f.delete("b"), f.delete("nope"), f.free_runs())[5] == [(0, 7)])(ExtentFS(7))

+++ problem | TLB, page table and Clock, all at once
--- task
Simulate address translation from end to end. Write `simulate(trace, page, tlb_size, frames, levels, mem_ns, tlb_ns, fault_ns)`. `trace` is a list of virtual addresses; pages are `page` bytes.

- The **TLB** holds up to `tlb_size` translations and is managed LRU. A hit makes that page the most recently used entry.
- On a TLB miss, the **page table** is walked (it has `levels` levels). If the page is not resident, it is a **page fault**: load it into the next never-used frame (0, 1, 2, …), or, when all `frames` frames are used, into the frame chosen by **Clock** (lesson 12): the hand starts at frame 0; while the frame under the hand has its reference bit set, clear it and move on; replace the frame under the hand, then move the hand one on. The evicted page loses its page-table entry and its TLB entry. After the miss, the translation goes into the TLB, evicting the least recently used entry if it is full.
- Every access sets the reference bit of its frame, TLB hit or not.
- Cost of one access: `tlb_ns + mem_ns` always; plus `levels * mem_ns` on a TLB miss; plus `fault_ns` on a fault.

Return `(physical, hits, faults, total_ns)`: the list of physical addresses (frame × page + offset), the number of TLB hits, the number of page faults, and the total cost.
--- starter
def simulate(trace, page, tlb_size, frames, levels, mem_ns, tlb_ns, fault_ns):
    return list(trace), 0, 0, 0
--- solution
def simulate(trace, page, tlb_size, frames, levels, mem_ns, tlb_ns, fault_ns):
    tlb = []
    table = {}
    owner = [None] * frames
    ref = [0] * frames
    hand = 0
    used = 0
    hits = faults = 0
    total = 0
    physical = []
    for addr in trace:
        vpn, offset = divmod(addr, page)
        total += tlb_ns + mem_ns
        if vpn in tlb:
            hits += 1
            tlb.remove(vpn)
        else:
            total += levels * mem_ns
            if vpn not in table:
                faults += 1
                total += fault_ns
                if used < frames:
                    frame = used
                    used += 1
                else:
                    while ref[hand]:
                        ref[hand] = 0
                        hand = (hand + 1) % frames
                    frame = hand
                    hand = (hand + 1) % frames
                    old = owner[frame]
                    del table[old]
                    if old in tlb:
                        tlb.remove(old)
                owner[frame] = vpn
                table[vpn] = frame
            if len(tlb) == tlb_size:
                tlb.pop(0)
        tlb.append(vpn)
        frame = table[vpn]
        ref[frame] = 1
        physical.append(frame * page + offset)
    return physical, hits, faults, total
--- check case | Hits, misses, faults and a Clock eviction that removes a TLB entry
simulate([0, 5, 100, 4, 260, 0, 512, 100, 768, 5], 256, 2, 3, 2, 100, 1, 10000)
=> ([0, 5, 100, 4, 260, 0, 512, 100, 0, 261], 5, 5, 52010)
--- check case | One page, many accesses
simulate([10, 20, 30], 64, 4, 4, 1, 50, 2, 0)
=> ([10, 20, 30], 2, 1, 206)
--- check case | A TLB miss on a resident page costs a walk but no fault
simulate([0, 64, 128, 0], 64, 2, 4, 3, 10, 1, 1000)
=> ([0, 64, 128, 0], 0, 3, 3164)
--- check case | Clock skips a recently used frame
simulate([0, 1, 2, 3, 1, 4], 1, 1, 3, 1, 1, 0, 0)
=> ([0, 1, 2, 0, 1, 2], 0, 5, 12)
--- check case | Nothing to translate
simulate([], 4096, 16, 8, 4, 100, 1, 5000)
=> ([], 0, 0, 0)

+++ problem | Recover the journal, then resolve paths
--- task
After a crash, a tiny file system must replay its journal before it can answer lookups. Write `recover_and_resolve(dirs, journal, paths)`.

- `dirs` maps each directory's inode number to its entries (name to inode number, including `"."` and `".."`); the root is inode 1. Any other inode number is a regular file. Do not change `dirs`.
- `journal` is a list of records: `("begin", tx)`, then any number of operations, then `("commit", tx)`. The operations are `("link", d, name, inum)` (add or replace an entry in directory `d`), `("unlink", d, name)` (remove it) and `("mkdir", parent, name, inum)` (create directory `inum` with `"."` and `".."` entries, and link it into `parent` under `name`).
- Replay from the start: each transaction whose operations are followed by a commit record with the **same** id is applied, in order. Stop at the first transaction that is incomplete or whose commit id does not match, or at any record that does not fit this pattern.
- Then resolve every path in `paths` (all absolute; empty parts are skipped) in the recovered file system. Return the list of inode numbers, with `"ENOENT"` for a missing name and `"ENOTDIR"` when a part is looked up in a regular file.
--- starter
def recover_and_resolve(dirs, journal, paths):
    return ["ENOENT" for _ in paths]
--- solution
def replay(dirs, journal):
    dirs = {d: dict(entries) for d, entries in dirs.items()}
    i = 0
    while i < len(journal) and journal[i][0] == "begin":
        tx = journal[i][1]
        j = i + 1
        while j < len(journal) and journal[j][0] in ("link", "unlink", "mkdir"):
            j += 1
        if j == len(journal) or journal[j] != ("commit", tx):
            break
        for record in journal[i + 1:j]:
            if record[0] == "link":
                _, d, name, inum = record
                dirs[d][name] = inum
            elif record[0] == "unlink":
                _, d, name = record
                del dirs[d][name]
            else:
                _, parent, name, inum = record
                dirs[inum] = {".": inum, "..": parent}
                dirs[parent][name] = inum
        i = j + 1
    return dirs


def resolve(dirs, path):
    current = 1
    for part in path.split("/"):
        if part == "":
            continue
        if current not in dirs:
            return "ENOTDIR"
        if part not in dirs[current]:
            return "ENOENT"
        current = dirs[current][part]
    return current


def recover_and_resolve(dirs, journal, paths):
    fixed = replay(dirs, journal)
    return [resolve(fixed, p) for p in paths]
--- check case | Two committed transactions and one with a stale commit id
recover_and_resolve({1: {".": 1, "..": 1, "etc": 2}, 2: {".": 2, "..": 1, "cfg": 5}}, [("begin", 1), ("mkdir", 1, "logs", 3), ("link", 3, "run.log", 6), ("commit", 1), ("begin", 2), ("link", 2, "cfg.new", 7), ("unlink", 2, "cfg"), ("commit", 2), ("begin", 3), ("unlink", 3, "run.log"), ("commit", 2)], ["/logs/run.log", "/etc/cfg", "/etc/cfg.new", "/logs/..", "/etc/cfg.new/x", "/nope"])
=> [6, "ENOENT", 7, 1, "ENOTDIR", "ENOENT"]
--- check case | An incomplete transaction stops the replay, even if later ones look complete
recover_and_resolve({1: {".": 1, "..": 1}}, [("begin", 1), ("link", 1, "a", 4), ("begin", 2), ("link", 1, "b", 5), ("commit", 2)], ["/a", "/b"])
=> ["ENOENT", "ENOENT"]
--- check case | A link replaces an existing entry (an atomic rename)
recover_and_resolve({1: {".": 1, "..": 1, "cfg": 5, "tmp": 6}}, [("begin", 9), ("link", 1, "cfg", 6), ("unlink", 1, "tmp"), ("commit", 9)], ["/cfg", "/tmp", "//cfg/"])
=> [6, "ENOENT", 6]
--- check test | The original directories are not changed
(lambda d: (recover_and_resolve(d, [("begin", 1), ("link", 1, "x", 3), ("commit", 1)], ["/x"]), d) == ([3], {1: {".": 1, "..": 1}}))({1: {".": 1, "..": 1}})
--- check case | An empty journal, and the root itself
recover_and_resolve({1: {".": 1, "..": 1, "f": 2}}, [], ["/", "/f", "/f/g"])
=> [1, 2, "ENOTDIR"]

+++ problem | C-LOOK with deadlines
--- task
Combine a disk scheduler with the aging idea of lesson 4. Write `serve(head, arrivals, deadline)`. The head starts at cylinder `head` at time 0 and moves one cylinder per millisecond; serving takes no extra time. `arrivals` is a list of `(time, cylinder)`.

Whenever the head is free, it picks one request among those that have arrived by now and are not yet served:

- If any of them has waited at least `deadline` ms (now minus its arrival time), pick the one that arrived first (ties: the lower cylinder).
- Otherwise use C-LOOK, always sweeping upwards: the lowest cylinder at or above the head's position (ties: the earlier arrival); if there is none, the lowest cylinder of all.

The head then travels there; requests that arrive during the trip wait until it arrives. If nothing is waiting, the head stays put until the next arrival. Return a list of `(cylinder, arrival_time, finish_time)` in service order.
--- starter
def serve(head, arrivals, deadline):
    return [(c, t, t) for t, c in arrivals]
--- solution
def serve(head, arrivals, deadline):
    future = sorted(arrivals, key=lambda r: r[0])
    waiting = []
    served = []
    now, pos, i = 0, head, 0
    while i < len(future) or waiting:
        while i < len(future) and future[i][0] <= now:
            waiting.append(future[i])
            i += 1
        if not waiting:
            now = future[i][0]
            continue
        late = [r for r in waiting if now - r[0] >= deadline]
        if late:
            pick = min(late, key=lambda r: (r[0], r[1]))
        else:
            ahead = [r for r in waiting if r[1] >= pos]
            pick = min(ahead or waiting, key=lambda r: (r[1], r[0]))
        waiting.remove(pick)
        now += abs(pick[1] - pos)
        pos = pick[1]
        served.append((pick[1], pick[0], now))
    return served
--- check case | Plain C-LOOK: up, then jump to the lowest
serve(50, [(0, 60), (0, 40), (3, 45)], 1000)
=> [(60, 0, 10), (40, 0, 30), (45, 3, 35)]
--- check case | Without a deadline, a stream of near requests keeps the far one waiting
serve(0, [(0, 90)] + [(k, 10 + k) for k in range(0, 60, 5)], 1000)[-1]
=> (90, 0, 90)
--- check case | With a deadline of 30 ms, the far request is served as soon as it is late
serve(0, [(0, 90)] + [(k, 10 + k) for k in range(0, 60, 5)], 30)[:7]
=> [(10, 0, 10), (15, 5, 15), (20, 10, 20), (25, 15, 25), (30, 20, 30), (90, 0, 90), (35, 25, 145)]
--- check case | The jump back goes to the lowest waiting cylinder
serve(10, [(0, 30), (0, 5), (1, 12)], 1000)
=> [(30, 0, 20), (5, 0, 45), (12, 1, 52)]
--- check case | Idle until the first arrival; no requests at all
(serve(20, [(5, 10)], 3), serve(5, [], 10))
=> ([(10, 5, 15)], [])

+++ problem | fork against pids.max
--- task
Model the cgroup limit that stops fork bombs. Write a class `PidCgroups`. `PidCgroups(limits)` gets a dict from cgroup path (`"/"`, `"/ct"`, `"/ct/web"`, …) to its `pids.max`; a path that is missing has no limit. Pids are handed out 1, 2, 3, … and never reused.

- `count(path)`: the number of processes in that cgroup **and all cgroups below it** (every process counts for `"/"`).
- `start(path)`: create a process in `path`, as a container runtime does for a container's first process. `fork(pid)`: create a child of `pid` in the same cgroup. Either one fails with `-EAGAIN` (−11), creating nothing, if any cgroup from `path` up to `"/"` already has `count` at or above its limit. Otherwise return the new pid.
- `exit(pid)`: the process is gone.
- `move(pid, path)`: move a process into another cgroup. As in Linux, limits are checked only when a process is created, so a move always succeeds, even into a cgroup that is at its limit.
--- starter
EAGAIN = 11


class PidCgroups:
    def __init__(self, limits):
        self.limits = limits
        self.next_pid = 1

    def count(self, path):
        return 0

    def start(self, path):
        self.next_pid += 1
        return self.next_pid - 1

    def fork(self, pid):
        return self.start("/")

    def exit(self, pid):
        pass

    def move(self, pid, path):
        pass
--- solution
EAGAIN = 11


class PidCgroups:
    def __init__(self, limits):
        self.limits = limits
        self.group = {}
        self.next_pid = 1

    def chain(self, path):
        out = [path]
        while path != "/":
            path = path.rsplit("/", 1)[0] or "/"
            out.append(path)
        return out

    def count(self, path):
        prefix = path.rstrip("/") + "/"
        return sum(1 for g in self.group.values() if path == "/" or g == path or g.startswith(prefix))

    def allowed(self, path):
        for p in self.chain(path):
            limit = self.limits.get(p)
            if limit is not None and self.count(p) >= limit:
                return False
        return True

    def start(self, path):
        if not self.allowed(path):
            return -EAGAIN
        pid = self.next_pid
        self.next_pid += 1
        self.group[pid] = path
        return pid

    def fork(self, pid):
        return self.start(self.group[pid])

    def exit(self, pid):
        del self.group[pid]

    def move(self, pid, path):
        self.group[pid] = path
--- check test | A child cgroup's limit, then the parent's
(lambda c: (c.start("/ct/web"), c.fork(1), c.fork(1), c.start("/ct"), c.count("/ct"), c.count("/"), c.count("/ct/web")) == (1, 2, -11, 3, 3, 3, 2))(PidCgroups({"/ct": 3, "/ct/web": 2, "/": 10}))
--- check test | A failed fork uses up no pid; exit makes room again
(lambda c: (c.start("/ct/web"), c.fork(1), c.start("/ct"), c.fork(1), c.exit(2), c.fork(1), c.count("/ct")) == (1, 2, 3, -11, None, 4, 3))(PidCgroups({"/ct": 3, "/ct/web": 2}))
--- check test | Moving is always allowed, and the moved process then counts where it went
(lambda c: (c.start("/ct"), c.start("/"), c.move(2, "/ct"), c.count("/ct"), c.fork(1), c.fork(2), c.start("/other"), c.count("/")) == (1, 2, None, 2, -11, -11, 3, 3))(PidCgroups({"/ct": 1}))
--- check test | "/ct" does not count processes in "/ctx"
(lambda c: (c.start("/ctx"), c.start("/ct"), c.count("/ct"), c.count("/ctx"), c.start("/ct")) == (1, 2, 1, 1, -11))(PidCgroups({"/ct": 1}))
--- check test | No limits at all
(lambda c: ([c.start("/a/b") for _ in range(50)][-1], c.count("/a"), c.count("/a/b/c")) == (50, 50, 0))(PidCgroups({}))

+++ question | How many processes?
--- ask
How many processes exist at the end, counting the original one, if no process exits?

```cpp fragment
fork();
if (fork() == 0)
    fork();
```

Type a whole number.
--- answer
6
--- why
The first `fork` makes 2 processes. Each of them runs `fork()` again: in each pair, the child (where `fork` returned 0) forks once more. So each of the 2 processes becomes 3 (itself, its child, and that child's child): 6 in all.

+++ question | Root inside a container
--- ask
A container runs as root without a user namespace. Its process escapes the mount namespace through a kernel bug. What privileges does it have on the host?
--- choice
None: namespaces make root inside a container an ordinary user outside.
--- choice correct
Full root privileges, because without a user namespace its user id 0 is the host's user id 0.
--- choice
Only the privileges its cgroup's limits allow.
--- choice
It is killed at once by the hypervisor.
--- why
Only a user namespace maps the container's root to an unprivileged user on the host. Without one, uid 0 inside is uid 0 outside, and cgroups limit amounts (CPU, memory, pids), not privileges. That is why rootless containers and user namespaces matter for isolation.

+++ question | What lock ordering breaks
--- ask
Every thread acquires mutexes in increasing order of their address. Which of the four deadlock conditions does that rule make impossible?
--- choice
Mutual exclusion
--- choice
Hold and wait
--- choice
No preemption
--- choice correct
Circular wait
--- why
A cycle in the wait-for graph would need some thread to hold a higher-numbered lock while waiting for a lower one, which the ordering forbids. Threads still hold one lock while waiting for another (hold and wait), and locks are still exclusive and not preemptible.

+++ question | A cycle with single instances
--- ask
In a resource-allocation graph where every resource has exactly one instance, the graph has a cycle. What follows?
--- choice
The system is unsafe, but may still avoid deadlock.
--- choice correct
The threads on the cycle are deadlocked.
--- choice
Nothing, unless the cycle includes every thread.
--- choice
A deadlock will happen only if another request arrives.
--- why
With one instance per resource, each thread on the cycle waits for a resource held by the next, and none can be satisfied by any other instance. So a cycle is both necessary and sufficient for deadlock. With several instances per resource, a cycle is necessary but not sufficient.

+++ question | What best fit leaves behind
--- ask
Why does best fit tend to leave many tiny unusable holes in memory?
--- choice
It always splits the largest hole.
--- choice correct
It picks the hole that fits most tightly, so the leftover piece it splits off is as small as possible, often too small for any later request.
--- choice
It never coalesces neighbouring free holes.
--- choice
It allocates from the end of memory backwards.
--- why
Choosing the tightest fit minimises the remainder of each split, and those remainders pile up as slivers. That is external fragmentation: plenty of free memory in total, but no single hole big enough. Coalescing on free helps, and is independent of the placement policy.

+++ question | Effective access time
--- ask
A TLB lookup takes a negligible time, a memory access takes 100 ns, and the page table has one level. The TLB hit rate is 90%. What is the effective access time in nanoseconds? Type a whole number.
--- answer
110
110 ns
--- why
A hit costs one memory access (100 ns). A miss costs the page-table read plus the access (200 ns). 0.9 × 100 + 0.1 × 200 = 90 + 20 = 110 ns.

+++ question | A flat page table
--- ask
A machine has 32-bit virtual addresses, 4 KiB pages and 4-byte page-table entries. How many MiB does one flat (single-level) page table take for one process? Type a whole number.
--- answer
4
4 MiB
--- why
4 KiB pages leave 32 − 12 = 20 bits of page number, so 2²⁰ entries. At 4 bytes each that is 2²² bytes = 4 MiB per process, even for a process that uses a few pages. Multi-level tables avoid allocating the unused parts.

+++ question | Belady's anomaly
--- ask
Which replacement policy can have **more** page faults when it is given **more** frames?
--- choice correct
FIFO
--- choice
LRU
--- choice
OPT
--- choice
None of them: more memory never hurts.
--- why
LRU and OPT are stack algorithms: the pages resident with n frames are always a subset of those resident with n + 1 frames, so more frames can never add a fault. FIFO has no such property, and the classic reference string 1, 2, 3, 4, 1, 2, 5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 has 9 faults with 3 frames and 10 with 4.

+++ question | Links after a rename
--- ask
`data.csv` has a hard link `copy.csv` and a symbolic link `latest.csv` (holding the path `data.csv`). Then `data.csv` is renamed to `old.csv`. Which names still reach the file's contents?
--- choice
All three names.
--- choice correct
`old.csv` and `copy.csv`; `latest.csv` now dangles.
--- choice
Only `old.csv`.
--- choice
`old.csv` and `latest.csv`; the hard link breaks.
--- why
A hard link is a directory entry holding the inode number, so it does not care what other names the inode has. A symbolic link holds a path, and the path `data.csv` no longer exists after the rename, so resolving `latest.csv` fails with ENOENT.

+++ question | Ordered journaling
--- ask
ext4's default "ordered" mode journals only metadata. What does it guarantee that "writeback" mode (also metadata only, with no ordering of data) does not?
--- choice
File data is written twice, so it survives any crash.
--- choice correct
Data blocks reach the disk before the metadata that points to them commits, so after a crash a file never points at blocks holding stale garbage.
--- choice
Every write is durable as soon as `write` returns.
--- choice
Recovery never needs to replay the journal.
--- why
In ordered mode the data is flushed to its home location before the transaction with the inode and bitmap changes commits. In writeback mode the metadata can commit first, and a crash can leave an inode pointing at blocks that still hold another file's old contents. Neither mode makes `write` durable without `fsync`.

+++ question | Why flash drives skip the elevator
--- ask
Why does Linux usually use no reordering scheduler ("none") for NVMe SSDs?
--- choice
SSDs cannot accept more than one request at a time.
--- choice correct
An SSD has no seek or rotation, so sorting requests by address saves almost nothing, and the drive's many internal queues reorder better than the host could.
--- choice
Reordering would increase write amplification to infinity.
--- choice
SSDs require requests in strictly increasing address order.
--- why
Disk scheduling exists to cut seek and rotational delays. Flash has neither: a random read costs about the same as a sequential one. The drive's controller spreads requests over many flash chips itself, so host-side sorting only adds CPU work and latency.

+++ question | Close with notify_one
--- ask
A bounded queue's `close()` sets `closed = true` and calls `not_empty.notify_one()`. Three consumer threads are blocked in `pop()` on the empty queue. What happens?
--- choice
All three wake and return "no more items".
--- choice correct
One consumer wakes and returns; the other two stay asleep for ever, and joining them hangs the program.
--- choice
The program crashes, because `notify_one` cannot be called while `closed` is true.
--- choice
Nothing wakes, because notifications while holding the mutex are lost.
--- why
`notify_one` wakes one waiter. Closing changes the condition for every waiter, and nobody else will ever notify again, so the other two sleep for ever. `close()` must use `notify_all`. A notification sent while holding the mutex is not lost: the woken thread simply waits to reacquire it.
